Methodology

How we calculate

Every number in CarFinder is a list price from a published source, a dealer band set per model, and a stack of taxes and fees from the rules of the region you pick. This page shows all of it: the basis per country, the bands, every fee table with its source, the exchange rates and their date, what is an estimate, and how to correct a figure.

It is generated by the build from the same data files the app ships with, so it cannot drift from the numbers, and the coverage figures on it are measured at every build. Car prices as of 29 September 2026, aircraft as of 12 October 2026, exchange rates of 14 October 2026.

The three prices

Every row shows the same three numbers, built the same way in every tab.

  1. List price. The maker’s published price for the cheapest and the dearest trim, variant or package of the model, on the basis that market uses (US sticker before destination, Indian ex-showroom, UK on-the-road, Australian RRP before on-roads, Canadian MSRP before freight, and so on; each tab names its basis). Nothing is adjusted: if a brand quotes a price with tax inside, so does the row.
  2. Typical dealer price. A band, not a quote: each model carries a market level (hot, steady, deals) set from the discount climate for that model in that market when the data was compiled, and the band is a share of the list price (the table below). The breakdown applies the band to the trim you pick with the slider.
  3. On-road price. The dealer band plus every mandatory cost of putting the vehicle on the road, water or ramp in the region you pick: destination or freight, taxes, registration, plates, compulsory insurance, levies and rebates, each as a separate line with a note on what it is. Lines marked shown, not added (insurance where it is optional, running costs, mooring) are information only. A total is a range because the dealer price is a range.

Rounding. Prices are stored in whole local units (rupees, not lakh; yuan, not 万). Fees are computed exactly and displayed rounded to the unit; a ~ marks a typical figure rather than a statutory one. Price presets and wizard budgets are rounded to two significant figures. Nothing is rounded before it is added.

Running costs. Yearly fuel or charging uses the official efficiency figure (EPA, ARAI, WLTP, NRCan, CLTC, Eco Sticker, INMETRO…) at the fuel and electricity prices in the cost assumptions, which you can edit; monthly payments use the loan terms there. Aircraft and boats use fuel burn per hour times the hours you set.

Data as of

Compiled is the date of a whole dataset; a row’s date is its dataset’s date, and there are no per-row verification dates yet. Every row is in exactly one of four states, each true of the row and nothing more: checked, a person compared the row to the maker’s page after compile (none yet; this is where per-row verification goes); listed, the maker’s published list price for this market on the compile date, not individually re-checked since; converted, a published list in another currency converted at a dated exchange rate (aircraft outside the US); estimated, modelled from announcements, trade press or dealer quotes (most boats, and bikes outside the US). The card and the detail sheet show the state; the masthead counts the converted and estimated rows on every tab.

DatasetPrices compiledTax and fee rules as ofRates on the compile dayConverted atStates

Converted and estimated rows are included by default and tagged on the card; the filter panel’s “Include the … with converted or estimated prices” box turns them off (the permalink carries est=0).

Exchange rates

The World tab converts every list price and on-road figure at the ECB euro foreign exchange reference rates of 14 October 2026, the latest pull; rubles use the Bank of Russia official rate of the same day, because the ECB has not published a ruble reference rate since March 2022. Aircraft list prices outside the US are converted once, at the rates of the day they were compiled (12 October 2026), and stay fixed in local currency until the next compile. The table keeps every rate the data has used, per US dollar, and the last column is how far each currency has moved against the dollar between the car list’s compile day (29 September 2026) and the World tab’s rates (positive: the currency strengthened, so a price converted from it is now worth more): that is the gap a converted price can carry, and the detail sheet states it for the row’s own currency against the currency you picked whenever it exceeds half a percent.

Re-pull the rates for a new compile with node tools/fx.mjs YYYY-MM-DD (ECB 90-day history and the Bank of Russia daily XML into data/sources.json), set fx.date and the dataset’s compiledAt or convertedAt, regenerate the aircraft rows with python3 tools/air.py, then node src/build.js.

What is measured

The trust layer is measured, not asserted: src/quality.js computes these figures from the data files at every build, writes them to docs/DATA-QUALITY.md and one row per release to data-quality-history.csv, and the test suite fails when a number drops below its floor in data/quality-floor.json. The floors are per vertical, with a few points of margin, so adding boats cannot break cars and a vertical that stands still is visible.

Cars and trucks

Price basis: Manufacturer's suggested retail price (MSRP) by trim, 2026 model year. Compiled: 29 September 2026 from Manufacturer build-and-price pages (the maker link on each row) not linked · EPA fuel-economy ratings (fueleconomy.gov) · Cox Automotive / Kelley Blue Book incentive reports (dealer bands)

How these numbers work

Sticker (MSRP) is the manufacturer’s base price for each model’s cheapest trim through its most expensive, before the mandatory destination charge. Prices are the 2026 model year as published by the manufacturers, compiled September 29, 2026, with 2025 leftovers and early 2027 models included and labeled. Models marked Estimate use announced or expected pricing that can still change; Listed rows are the maker’s published list price on the compile date.

Estimated drive-off starts from the sticker plus destination, applies a typical dealer discount or markup for that model’s demand level (in August 2026 incentives averaged about 6.6% of MSRP industry-wide, with far larger EV incentives), then adds your state’s sales tax, a typical dealer documentation fee, and roughly $350 for title and plates. Local tax rates, dealer add-ons and negotiation change the real number, so treat it as a range to bargain within.

Fuel economy is the EPA combined rating for the most efficient common configuration; electric range is the EPA figure for the longest-range trim. The federal $7,500 EV tax credit ended September 30, 2025; some states and utilities still offer EV rebates.

State table (data/states_us.json)

Combined state and average local sales-tax rate on a vehicle purchase, the typical dealer documentation fee, and the note the breakdown shows. Title and plates are about $350 nationwide. The Source column is the state revenue department, DMV or statute page the rate was read from, independent of this table; doc fees are the statutory cap where one exists and otherwise a typical figure from dealer surveys, so they are the least certain number in the row.

Tax name and trade-in credit (data/us_tax.json)

What the state calls the tax and whether it is charged on the price less a trade-in. Full: the trade-in comes off the taxable amount; none: tax on the full price; a dollar figure: the credit is capped at that trade-in value; no tax: nothing to reduce. Enter a trade-in under Cost assumptions and the breakdown applies the rule and shows the tax it saves; with none entered it says what a $15,000 trade would save. South Carolina’s fee is capped at $500, so a trade-in only changes it when the difference is under $10,000; Connecticut’s 7.75% band is set by the price, the tax by the price less the trade-in. DC charges its excise on the NADA value by weight class and city mpg (the weight class is estimated from body, size and fuel). Rules checked 17 October 2026; Sales Tax Handbook is the compiled source except where a state document is cited (it was out of date for Maryland, Kentucky and Michigan). Each state also has a page: car sales tax by state and trade-in credit by state.

Local rates by city and county (data/us_local.json)

Where a vehicle’s rate depends on the place it is registered, the state’s own table of combined state and local rates, copied on the date shown and checksummed against the published table. Pick a city or county under the state menu; anything not in the table uses the state’s average from the table above, and the breakdown says so. California: 541 places, 7.25% to 11.75%, effective 1 October 2026, from CDTFA, California City and County Sales and Use Tax Rates (effective 1 October 2026). New York: 81 places, 7% to 8.875%, effective 1 March 2025, from NY Department of Taxation and Finance, Publication 718 (effective 1 March 2025). Other states with county or city taxes (Illinois, Washington, Arizona, Colorado, Louisiana, Missouri, Alabama, Nevada, Tennessee, Utah, Oklahoma and more) still use a typical combined rate; their tables are next. Texas does not apply local sales tax to motor vehicles.

Motorcycles and scooters

Price basis: MSRP before freight and setup, 2026 and early 2027 model years. Compiled: 10 October 2026 from Manufacturer sites and dealer showroom listings not linked · State DMV fee schedules (docs/tax-notes/us-bikes-boats.md)

How these numbers work

List price is the maker’s published price for each model’s cheapest variant through its most expensive, on the basis used locally (MSRP before freight and setup), compiled in early October 2026. Rows marked Estimate use estimated or unconfirmed pricing; Listed rows are the maker’s published price on the compile date.

Estimated ride-away applies the discount typical for that model’s demand, then adds freight and setup where they are charged, the sales, purchase or road tax for your region, registration and plates, and the compulsory insurance where it is bought with the bike. Full insurance is shown as information. Dealer-fitted extras and accessories are not included.

Fuel economy is the maker’s or the official combined figure; electric range is the claimed figure for the biggest battery. Licence classes, learner restrictions and city bans are noted per model where they matter.

liability cover is compulsory in 48 states; full cover for a 35 to 50-year-old: cruisers and standards $600–$900, sportbikes $900–$1,500, riders under 25 up to $3,000		Information: ~$600 – $1,500 (not Trike)shown, not added

three-wheelers are cheaper to insure than two-wheelers		Information: ~$500 – $900 (only Trike)shown, not added
Rider course (optional)
MSF Basic RiderCourse $0–$350; waives the road test in most states and earns 5% to 15% off insurance		Information: $0 – $350shown, not added

Local terms: M endorsement: The motorcycle class added to a US driver licence after a written and road test, or an MSF rider course. No engine-size ladder for adults: pass once and any bike is legal. Autocycle: Three-wheelers such as the Polaris Slingshot are "autocycles" in about 45 states (a car licence is enough); Can-Am Spyder and Ryker and Harley trikes count as motorcycles in many states and need the M endorsement. DCT / E-Clutch: Honda’s dual-clutch automatic (no clutch lever) and its E-Clutch, which works the clutch for you while you still shift gears. Twist-and-go scooters use a CVT instead. Helmet laws: Universal helmet laws in 19 states plus DC; most other states require helmets under 18 or 21; Illinois, Iowa and New Hampshire have none.

Boats and jet skis

Price basis: MSRP for the standard package (boat, motor and trailer where sold that way). Compiled: 10 October 2026 from Manufacturer sites and dealer listings not linked · State boat sales-tax caps and registration fees (docs/tax-notes/us-bikes-boats.md)

How these numbers work

List price is the brand’s published price for each model’s cheapest standard package through its most expensive, compiled in early October 2026. In this market many boats are priced as boat-motor-trailer packages and others as a hull with the engine extra; the note on each row says which. Rows marked Estimate use estimated or unconfirmed pricing; Listed rows are the maker’s published price on the compile date.

Estimated on-the-water applies the discount typical for that model, then adds freight and dealer preparation where they are charged, the sales or purchase tax for your region (with the caps some places apply to boats), registration and titling, and the compulsory insurance where any exists. Trailer registration, slips, mooring and full insurance are shown as information.

Fuel use is a typical cruise figure in gallons or litres per hour for the standard engine; the yearly fuel estimate multiplies it by the engine hours you set in the cost assumptions.

Local terms: BMT / national price: Boat, motor and trailer sold as one priced package. The Tracker group (Tracker, Sun Tracker, Nitro, Mako, Tahoe, Ranger) sells at a fixed "national price" that dealers do not haggle on; freight and prep are added. Boater education card: No federal licence, but almost every state requires a safety course for operators born after a cut-off year (Florida 1988, Texas 1993, Georgia 1998) or for everyone (California, New York). Online NASBLA courses cost $0–$60. USCG documentation: Optional federal registration for boats of 5 net tons and over (roughly 27 ft up): about $133 to start and $26 a year; lenders often require it above $50,000. Engine cut-off switch: Since 2021 federal law requires the lanyard on planing boats under 26 ft; on a PWC it must be worn at all times. Boat excise / property tax: Massachusetts charges $10 per $1,000 of value a year; Connecticut, Maine, South Carolina, Virginia, Missouri and Kansas levy personal-property tax on boats. Shown as information, not added.

Planes, jets and helicopters

Price basis: Manufacturer factory list price, base to typically equipped, 2026. Compiled: 12 October 2026 from Manufacturer price lists and type announcements not linked · GAMA shipment and billing reports · AIN (Aviation International News) · Flying Magazine

How these numbers work

Factory list price is the maker’s 2026 price for each type from the base configuration to a typically equipped one, compiled in October 2026 from manufacturer price lists, type announcements and the trade press. US prices are the maker’s dollar list before sales or use tax. Business jets and helicopters are quoted typically equipped; the spread in each row covers the usual options.

Estimated delivered applies the discount or premium typical for that type (delivery positions on hot types trade above list; slow sellers and end-of-run types go for 5% to 12% under), then adds delivery and acceptance where the maker charges it, the sales tax, VAT, GST or duty for your region with the exemptions and caps that apply to aircraft, and registration with the national authority. Insurance, hangarage, the annual inspection and a pilot licence are shown as information, since they dwarf the purchase taxes over the years you own it.

Fuel burn is the maker’s cruise figure in gallons or litres per hour; the yearly fuel estimate multiplies it by the flight hours you set in the cost assumptions at local avgas and Jet-A prices. Speed and range are the maker’s long-range cruise figures in knots and nautical miles, with km or miles alongside.

Local terms: Factory list vs typically equipped: Makers quote a base price and a "typically equipped" price with the options most buyers take; the row’s range spans both. Delivery position: New aircraft are sold as positions in the production line; a hot type means a wait of one to three years, and positions trade at a premium. Pre-buy / acceptance inspection: An independent inspection before you accept delivery, even on a new aircraft; catches squawks the factory should fix at its cost. Registration: Every aircraft is registered with the national authority (FAA N-number, CAA G-register, EASA state registers, DGCA VT-, CAAC B-); the fee is small, the paperwork is not. Owner-flown: Aircraft a licensed private pilot can fly without a professional crew: pistons, most turboprops and a few single-pilot jets (Vision Jet, Citation M2, Phenom 100). eVTOL: Electric vertical take-off aircraft; the EHang EH216-S is certified in China and ultralight single-seaters fly in the US, while the air-taxi designs (Joby, Archer, VX4) are still in certification and not sold to individuals. LSA / MOSAIC: Light-sport aircraft: simple two-seaters flown on a sport-pilot licence; the FAA’s 2025 MOSAIC rule lets heavier four-seaters qualify. TBO: Time between overhauls of the engine, 1,500 to 2,200 hours on pistons; an overhaul costs US$30,000 to US$60,000 and is part of the running cost. Fly-away exemption: Texas, Florida, Kansas and others exempt a sale from sales tax when the buyer flies the aircraft out of the state within a set number of days; your home state may then charge use tax.

Research note: United States (us): motorcycle and boat fees, October 2026 (57 sources)

Snapshot date 10 Oct 2026. Rates and fees are what a buyer meets in the 50 states plus DC; the state codes are the same two-letter codes CarFinder already uses for cars (data/states_us.json, plus US = nationwide average). Items marked "approx" were not confirmed on a government page and should be shown as estimates.

Motorcycles: price basis

  • price_min / price_max are the US MSRP of the cheapest and most expensive standard variant (for example base, ABS, SE, DCT trims), in whole dollars. MSRP excludes freight/destination, dealer set-up (PDI), the dealer doc fee, sales/use tax, title, plates and registration, and accessories. The typical freight plus set-up amount is in each row's freight field.
  • Harley-Davidson and Indian list a "Starting at" price for the base colour; price_max adds the usual premium-paint or trim upcharge (about $500 to $1,500) and is not a published variant price. Ducati, KTM and Zero show 2027 model-year prices for several bikes because those 2027 bikes went on sale in mid-2026; Honda, Yamaha, Kawasaki, Suzuki, BMW, Harley-Davidson, Indian, Triumph and Royal Enfield prices are 2026 model year.
  • est = 1 (50 of 270 rows) means the price is estimated or only partly confirmed (small importers, MV Agusta, Husqvarna, some Aprilia and Moto Guzzi, electric Kawasakis, Can-Am three-wheeler top trims). Tariffs on imported bikes in 2025 and 2026 are already inside these MSRPs.

Motorcycles: dealer discount climate

hot: 0% to +2% steady: -4% to 0% deals: -12% to -5%

  • hot (sells at sticker, some dealers add up to 2% market adjustment): Honda Rebel 300/500, Triumph Speed 400, Royal Enfield Hunter 350 and Classic 350, Yamaha MT-07 and R9, BMW R 1300 GS, Ducati Multistrada V4, Kawasaki Ninja 500 and Z900RS.
  • steady (small discounts, free accessories, financing offers): most Honda, Yamaha, Kawasaki, BMW, Triumph, Ducati and Royal Enfield models.
  • deals (factory rebates of $500 to $4,000 or 0.99% to 4.99% APR for 36 to 60 months, plus dealer discounts): Harley-Davidson (excluding CVO), Indian (especially baggers and Scouts), Suzuki, KTM and Husqvarna, Zero, LiveWire, Can-Am, Slingshot and 2026 leftovers once 2027 models arrive in autumn.

Motorcycles: licence classes and incentives

  • Licence. Every state requires a motorcycle licence or an M endorsement on the driver licence for anything above the moped limit (usually 50 cc / 30 mph / about 2 hp; California M2 covers scooters up to 150 cc). There is no engine-size ladder for adults in the US: once a rider passes the written test plus a road test (or the MSF Basic RiderCourse, which waives the road test in most states) any bike is allowed. Learner permits typically bar passengers, night riding and highways. Riders under 18 face engine-size or course rules in some states (CA requires a course for under-21 first-time riders).
  • Beginner-friendly picks in this dataset: Honda Rebel 300/500, Kawasaki Ninja 500/Z500/Eliminator, Yamaha MT-03/R3, Triumph Speed 400, KTM 390 Duke, Royal Enfield Hunter/Classic 350, Suzuki GSX-8S (all under about 50 hp or with ABS).
  • Three-wheelers. Polaris Slingshot is an "autocycle" in about 45 states (car licence only; helmet rules vary). Can-Am Spyder/Ryker and Harley trikes are motorcycles in many states (M endorsement) but not in all; check the state DMV.
  • Helmets. Universal helmet laws in 19 states plus DC (AL, CA, GA, LA, MD, MA, MS, MO, NE, NV, NJ, NY, NC, OR, TN, VT, VA, WA, WV); partial laws (usually under 18 or 21) elsewhere; IL, IA and NH have no helmet law.
  • Incentives. No federal credit for electric motorcycles: the 30D credit never covered two-wheelers and ended for all EVs on 30 Sep 2025. A few states run small rebates for e-motorcycles (for example Connecticut CHEAPR, Illinois, Colorado); amounts change, so CarFinder should show no incentive. Many states (for example CA, AZ, NV, TX, UT) let motorcycles use carpool lanes solo.

Boats: price basis

  • price_min / price_max are US MSRP for the standard package in dollars, before freight/prep/commissioning, sales tax, title, registration and dealer fees. The package is stated in each row's note.
  • BMT = boat, motor and trailer: Tracker, Nitro, Ranger, Mako skiffs (13-19 ft) and Triton are sold as complete BMT packages at a "national price" (no haggling, but clearance rebates); Lowe, Lund, Alumacraft, Crestliner, G3 and Smoker Craft aluminum boats are usually quoted as BMT too, at dealer prices. Sun Tracker, Tahoe, Bennington, larger Mako and the fiberglass brands include the outboard or inboard engine but not the trailer ($2,500-$4,500 single axle, $5,000-$9,000 tandem, $9,000-$14,000 for tritoons and 25+ ft hulls). Personal watercraft are ski only; a single-ski trailer is $1,000-$2,500. Wake boats usually include the trailer and ballast. Sailboats include the diesel auxiliary but not electronics, canvas or commissioning.
  • Prices follow the newest model year on the maker's site in October 2026: model-year 2027 for Sea-Doo, Yamaha WaveRunner, Kawasaki Jet Ski, the Tracker group (Tracker, Sun Tracker, Nitro, Mako 13-17 ft and 226/246, Tahoe, Ranger) and Bennington; model-year 2026 or earlier for the rest. est = 0 only for Sea-Doo, Yamaha WaveRunner, Kawasaki Jet Ski and the Tracker group (51 of 162 rows); est = 1 (111 rows) marks every other brand, including Bennington, whose starting prices are confirmed but whose top prices are estimated.
  • Typical engines are Mercury or Yamaha outboards; gph is an estimate of fuel burn at typical cruise with the standard engine, in US gallons per hour (not a test result).

Boats: dealer discount climate

hot: 0% to +1% steady: -6% to 0% deals: -15% to -6%

  • The 2025-26 boat market is soft: high interest rates, tariffs on imported parts and a large stock of 2025 and 2026 boats. Autumn is model-year-end clearance (Oct-Dec) and the best deals appear at winter boat shows (Jan-Feb).
  • hot: Grady-White, Boston Whaler (Montauk and Vantage) and Scout; premium saltwater brands rarely sell below MSRP and some dealers have order waits.
  • steady: Bennington, wake boats, most fiberglass bowriders, most Tracker-group "no haggle" boats (clearance rebates of $1,000-$3,000 are common), Sea-Doo (2026 models carry $1,000 off or 2 years of coverage), Yamaha WaveRunner and Kawasaki Jet Ski (rebate cycles of $500-$1,500).
  • deals: 2025 and 2026 leftovers (Mako 24 Apex was $10,500 off), Sun Tracker clearance, Tahoe, Mako, entry fiberglass bowriders and any boat with an expiring model year. Dealers often bundle trailer, cover or free winterisation instead of cutting price.

Boats: licences and rules

  • No federal boating licence. Education rules are state law, set by birth year, age or horsepower, and almost every state has some rule (approx; check the state agency). Examples: Florida needs a Boating Safety ID Card for anyone born on or after 1 Jan 1988 to run a boat of 10 hp or more; Texas requires a course for anyone born on or after 1 Sep 1993 who runs a boat over 15 hp or a PWC; North Carolina for anyone born after 1 Jan 1988 on 10 hp or more; Georgia for anyone born on or after 1 Jan 1998; California and New York now require a boater card or safety certificate for all operators of motorised boats. Online NASBLA-approved courses cost $0-$60.
  • Personal watercraft. Minimum operator age is 14 to 16 in most states (education card required), a wearable life jacket is mandatory, riding at night is banned, and the engine cut-off lanyard must be worn.
  • Horsepower. No limit for adult beginners. Many states restrict children under 12 to 10-15 hp or require an adult aboard; check the state.
  • Registration and title. Every motorised boat (and sailboat with an engine) must be registered in the state where it is mostly used, usually by the wildlife/natural-resources agency (TX TPWD, NC Wildlife, GA DNR, MD DNR, MN DNR) or the DMV (CA, NY, FL via county tax collector, MI Secretary of State). Registration numbers and a validation decal go on the bow, and the hull ID (HIN) on the transom. About 30 states also title boats. Federal documentation by the Coast Guard is optional for boats of 5 net tons and over (roughly 27 ft and up).
  • Safety rules (federal). A wearable life jacket for each person (children under 13 must wear one on a moving boat in most states), a throwable device on boats 16 ft and over, a fire extinguisher where required, sound signals, navigation lights, and flares on coastal waters. Since 2021 an engine cut-off switch must be used on planing boats under 26 ft. Boating under the influence is 0.08% blood alcohol.
  • Trailers need their own plate or registration in most states and must have lights; brakes are required above about 1,500-3,000 lb gross weight depending on the state.

Fuel and running assumptions

  • Road petrol: $4.45 per US gallon, or $1.18 per litre (CarFinder US default); premium (91-93 octane) about $5.15 per gallon ($1.36 per litre) for sport and luxury bikes.
  • Marine petrol at a marina: about $5.75 per gallon ($1.52 per litre); ethanol-free about $6.25 ($1.65 per litre). Marine diesel at a marina: about $5.90 per gallon ($1.56 per litre).
  • Home electricity: $0.17 per kWh (CarFinder US default).
  • Typical annual use: 3,000 miles (4,800 km) for a motorcycle (range 1,500-6,000; commuter scooters 4,000 and up); 50 engine hours a year for a pontoon, fishing or runabout boat, 40 for a PWC, 100-150 for a wake boat (including ballast time), 50 motoring hours for a sailboat.
  • Annual fuel cost = gph × hours × marine fuel price for boats; miles ÷ mpg × petrol price for bikes (mpg = 235.215 ÷ l100).
  • Typical loan: motorcycles 7.9% APR, 60 months, 10% down (manufacturer promotions run 0.99%-4.99% for 36-60 months); boats 8.5% APR, 180 months, 15% down (120 months under $30,000, 240 months over $100,000).
  • Depreciation, for information: a new motorcycle loses about 15%-20% in year 1; a new boat loses about 10%-15% in year 1 and 5%-8% a year afterwards. Maintenance on a boat is about 5%-10% of its value per year including storage.

Glossary (8–12 terms per vertical)

Motorcycles

  • MSRP: the manufacturer's suggested retail price, before freight, set-up, tax and fees.
  • Freight (destination): the charge for shipping the bike from the factory or port to the dealer.
  • PDI (set-up): the dealer's assembly and pre-delivery inspection, billed on top of the price.
  • Doc fee: the dealer's paperwork charge, capped by law in a few states.
  • M endorsement: the motorcycle class added to a driver licence after a test or rider course.
  • ABS: anti-lock brakes that stop the wheels locking under hard braking.
  • DCT: Honda's dual-clutch automatic gearbox, with no clutch lever.
  • E-Clutch: Honda's system that works the clutch for you but still lets you shift gears.
  • Cruiser, bagger: relaxed-posture bikes; baggers add a fairing and hard saddlebags.
  • Adventure bike: tall, long-travel bike made for both roads and unpaved tracks.
  • Dual-sport: a lightweight street-legal trail bike, usually 150 to 650 cc.
  • Autocycle/trike: three-wheeler (Slingshot, Spyder, Ryker, Harley trikes) with its own licence rules in many states.

Boats

  • BMT: boat, motor and trailer sold as one priced package.
  • National price: a fixed price (Tracker group) that dealers do not haggle on; freight and prep are added.
  • Freight and prep: the charge for delivering and commissioning a new boat.
  • Pontoon/tritoon: a deck on two (pontoon) or three (tritoon) aluminum tubes; tritoons are faster and take bigger engines.
  • Mod-V and deep-V: hull shapes; mod-V is flatter and stable for shallow lakes, deep-V cuts through big-water chop.
  • Center console: an open boat with the steering station in the middle, popular for fishing.
  • Outboard, sterndrive, inboard, jet drive: engine layouts; outboards hang off the transom, jets have no exposed propeller.
  • HIN: the hull identification number stamped on every boat, used for titling and registration.
  • Titling vs registration: title proves ownership; registration lets you operate on public water for a set period.
  • USCG documentation: optional federal registration for boats of 5 net tons or more.
  • Max HP/person capacity: the maximum engine power and number of people allowed on the manufacturer's capacity plate.
  • Wake/surf boat: an inboard tow boat with ballast tanks that shape a wave for surfing or wakeboarding.

Canada CA

Cars and trucks

Price basis: MSRP before freight and PDI. Compiled: 29 September 2026 from Manufacturer newsroom price releases not linked · Unhaggle · AutoTrader.ca research pages · Le Guide de l'auto · NRCan fuel-consumption ratings

How these numbers work

MSRP is the manufacturer’s Canadian sticker before freight, fees and taxes, for each model’s cheapest trim through its most expensive, from brand sites, Unhaggle and AutoTrader in late September 2026 (2027 model year where already on sale). Brands that only publish all-in prices are backed out and marked Estimate.

Estimated drive-away applies the discount typical for that model, then adds the brand’s freight & PDI, the A/C tax, tire and admin fees, the green levy and federal luxury tax where they apply, your province’s sales tax (BC’s price-tiered PST included), registration, and takes off the federal EVAP rebate and any provincial EV rebate. Insurance is shown but not included.

Consumption and range are NRCan ratings. September 2026 fuel prices reflect the Gulf shock (diesel at a record C$2.64) and the suspended federal excise tax.

Regions in the picker: Ontario · Quebec · British Columbia · Alberta · Manitoba · Saskatchewan · Nova Scotia · New Brunswick · Newfoundland & Labrador · Prince Edward Island · Territories (default Ontario). The rates, brackets and formulas for each are in the research note below.

Research note: Canada (ca) — on-road math and market notes, late September 2026 (47 sources)

Currency CAD ($). Prices in data/cars_ca.json are integer dollars, the manufacturer's suggested retail price (MSRP / PDSF) as published on the brand's Canadian site for the cheapest and dearest trim of each nameplate (options and paint excluded). Canadian MSRPs are quoted before freight & PDI, the $100 air-conditioner excise tax, the federal green levy, tire/environmental fees, dealer fees, the federal luxury tax and all sales taxes. They are NOT the "as shown", "starting at (all-in)" or "selling price" figures that Ontario/Quebec/BC/Alberta/Manitoba/Saskatchewan dealers must advertise (those add freight & PDI plus every fee, see 2.1). Where a brand only publishes all-in prices (Genesis, Tesla's "purchase price", some dealer sites) the MSRP was backed out and the row is flagged est=1. Efficiency columns: NRCan (Natural Resources Canada) 5-cycle combined L/100 km for the most efficient common variant and NRCan CO2 g/km for the variant behind price_min (gasoline ≈ 23.3 g/km per L/100 km, diesel ≈ 26.6); EV range is the NRCan (EPA-method) rating in km. Model year: 2026 for most rows, 2027 where the brand had already switched by 29 Sept 2026 (Toyota Corolla/Crown/bZ/C-HR/GR86/Prius, Kia EV5/Telluride, Chevrolet Bolt), 2025 leftovers only for discontinued models (Ford Escape, F-150 Lightning, VW ID.4/ID. Buzz, Jaguar F-Pace).

1. What the MSRP already includes

Nothing except the car and the manufacturer's margin. Unlike the UK/EU there is no tax inside the sticker, and unlike Australia the luxury tax is added at the till. Two federal levies that manufacturers often print on the window sticker (A/C tax, green levy) are still *outside* the MSRP. So:

`` pre-tax price = MSRP + freight&PDI + $100 A/C tax + green levy (if any) + tire fee + dealer admin fee + regulator fee (ON $22, AB $6.25) + options/accessories + federal luxury tax (if pre-tax price > $100,000) drive-away = pre-tax price × (1 + GST/HST + PST/QST rate for the province) + registration/plates (+ insurance down payment) `` Rule of thumb: drive-away ≈ MSRP × 1.16–1.22 in HST provinces (ON 13 %, Atlantic 14–15 %), × 1.21–1.24 in Quebec (14.975 %) and BC (12 %+), × 1.11 in Alberta and the territories (GST only). The freight & PDI charge alone adds 4–8 % to a $30k car.

Business buyers (GST/HST registrants) recover the GST/HST (capped at the $38,000 + tax capital-cost limit for passenger vehicles in 2026) — irrelevant to private buyers. Trade-ins reduce the taxable amount in every province except that in Quebec the QST/GST credit applies too (trade-in value is deducted before tax everywhere; this is worth 5–15 % of the trade-in value).

2. From MSRP to drive-away

2.2 Federal levies added to every sale
  • Air-conditioner excise tax: $100 on every vehicle with A/C (Excise Tax Act). Shown as a separate line, taxed with GST/HST/PST.
  • Green levy (excise tax on fuel-inefficient vehicles): based on the NRCan *weighted* rating (55 % city / 45 % highway) of automobiles — cars, station wagons, vans and SUVs with < 10 seats. Pickup trucks, ambulances, hearses and 10+ seat vans are exempt. Brackets: 13.0–13.99 L/100 km $1,000; 14.0–14.99 $2,000; 15.0–15.99 $3,000; 16.0 and above $4,000. Paid by the importer and passed through as "Federal Green Levy" (e.g. Escalade-V, Durango Hellcat, Wrangler 392, AMG G 63, Range Rover V8, Lamborghini Urus). Formula: levy = w<13 ? 0 : w<14 ? 1000 : w<15 ? 2000 : w<16 ? 3000 : 4000 where w = weighted L/100 km (≈ 0.55 × city + 0.45 × hwy; combined figures in the dataset run about 3–5 % below the weighted number, so treat combined ≥ 12.5 as a warning).
  • Select Luxury Items Tax (federal "luxury tax", since 1 Sept 2022): on new vehicles (< 10 seats, GVWR ≤ 3,856 kg, so most HD pickups and vans are exempt, as are motorhomes, ambulances, race cars and vehicles previously registered) whose taxable amount — MSRP + freight + options + accessories + dealer-installed extras, before GST/HST — exceeds $100,000. Tax = the lesser of 10 % of the whole taxable amount and 20 % of the amount above $100,000, i.e. lux = price<=100000 ? 0 : min(0.10*price, 0.20*(price-100000)); the 10 % cap only wins above $200,000. GST/HST (and QST/PST) are then charged on top of the luxury tax. Example: $130,000 Range Rover Sport → luxury tax $6,000; in Ontario HST 13 % on $136,000 = $17,680; $250,000 Bentley → $25,000 (10 % cap). Budget 2025 (4 Nov 2025) repealed the luxury tax on aircraft and boats but kept it on vehicles; nothing changed for 2026. Charged by the seller at delivery, not inside the MSRP.
  • Tariffs are inside the MSRP, not a line item. Since 9 April 2025 Canada applies a 25 % surtax on US-assembled vehicles (full value if not CUSMA-compliant; on the non-Canadian/Mexican content if CUSMA-compliant). A remission framework (renewed 9 April 2026 – 8 April 2027) waives it for automakers that keep Canadian production and investment (Ford, GM, Stellantis, Toyota, Honda; GM's and Stellantis's quotas were cut in Oct 2025 after CAMI and Brampton pauses). Brands without Canadian plants get no remission — Tesla, Rivian, Lucid, Subaru, Mazda, Nissan, Hyundai/Kia, VW, BMW, Mercedes, Volvo, Polestar — so they re-sourced (Tesla Model Y from Berlin, Model 3 from Shanghai, Santa Fe from Korea, Outback from Japan, Volvo EX30 from Belgium), dropped models (Subaru Ascent/Wilderness, Mazda CX-50, Lincoln Nautilus Hybrid for a year) or priced the tariff in (Rivian, Tesla Model S/X/Cybertruck, BMW X3/X5/X7, Mercedes GLE/GLS/EQ SUVs, Volvo EX90, Hyundai Ioniq 9, Kia EV9, VW Atlas/ID.4). US-built share of Canadian sales fell from 35.4 % (H1 2025) to 28.4 % (H1 2026); brands with no Canadian plant went from 17.7 % to 4.9 % US-sourced. Chinese-built EVs: the 100 % surtax was replaced on 1 March 2026 by the normal 6.1 % MFN duty inside an annual quota of 49,000 vehicles (24,500 per half-year, first-come); above the quota the 100 % surtax still applies. That is why the Shanghai-built Tesla Model 3 Premium RWD sells for $39,490, Polestar 2 and Lincoln Nautilus are back, and BYD is opening ~20 Canadian stores in late 2026 (no BYD pricing yet as of 29 Sept 2026). Chinese-built vehicles are not eligible for the federal EV rebate. On 8 Sept 2026 Canada added 15–50 % surtaxes on $27.6 bn of other US goods (steel, appliances, food); vehicles unchanged. The US side charges 25 % (s.232) on Canadian-built cars and has threatened to double it on 1 Jan 2027 — the reason Canadian-built RAV4/Civic/CR-V/Lexus RX/Pacifica/Charger/Silverado production is being redirected to the domestic market and why DesRosiers expects softer Q4 sales.

Programmer formulas (p = pre-tax price incl. freight, fees, luxury tax; zev = BEV/PHEV/FCEV): `` ON: tax = 0.13*p QC: tax = 0.05*p + 0.09975*p AB/YT/NT/NU: tax = 0.05*p SK: tax = 0.11*p MB: tax = 0.12*p NS: tax = 0.14*p NB/PE/NL: tax = 0.15*p BC: t = zev ? 20000 : 0 pst = p < 55000+t ? 0.07 : p < 56000+t ? 0.08 : p < 57000+t ? 0.09 : p < 125000 ? 0.10 : p < 150000 ? 0.15 : 0.20 tax = (0.05 + pst) * p `` Note the BC 15 %/20 % tiers start at $125,000/$150,000 for every vehicle (no ZEV uplift there). Private-sale rules matter only for used cars.

2.5 Registration, plates and compulsory insurance (first year)

Every province requires third-party liability (minimum $200,000; $50,000 in Quebec where SAAQ covers injuries) — private insurers in ON, AB, Atlantic Canada and the territories; public insurers in BC (ICBC), SK (SGI), MB (MPI) and, for injuries only, QC (SAAQ). Lenders and lessors demand collision + comprehensive too, so assume a full policy.

2.7 Regions for the dropdown (suggest 11)

Ontario (HST 13 %, free plate renewal, private insurance, dearest premiums) · Quebec (14.975 %, SAAQ + luxury 1 % surcharge, cheapest insurance, Roulez vert $2,000) · British Columbia (12 %+ with luxury PST tiers, ICBC public insurance, ZEV thresholds +$20k) · Alberta (GST only, private insurance, no incentives) · Manitoba (12 %, MPI public insurance) · Saskatchewan (11 %, SGI public insurance) · Nova Scotia (HST 14 %) · New Brunswick (HST 15 %) · Newfoundland & Labrador (HST 15 %, dearest fuel) · Prince Edward Island (HST 15 %, $4,000 provincial EV rebate) · Territories (GST 5 %, Yukon/NWT $5,000 EV rebates, highest fuel and insurance costs).

3. EV / hybrid incentives in force, September 2026

Federal — Electric Vehicle Affordability Program (EVAP), launched 16 Feb 2026 to replace iZEV (which ran out of money in Jan 2025):

  • $5,000 for a battery-electric or hydrogen vehicle, $2,500 for a plug-in hybrid, applied at the point of sale by the dealer (or Tesla/Rivian/Polestar online), on purchase or a lease of ≥ 12 months.
  • Cap: final transaction value ≤ $50,000 — MSRP + options + accessories + freight & PDI + dealer/admin fees, *before* taxes and rebates (extended warranties, winter tires, chargers and financing charges are excluded). A $1,300 tow hitch can push a $49,990 Model Y RWD over the line. Vehicles assembled in Canada have no price cap (Chrysler Pacifica PHEV and Dodge Charger Daytona from Windsor, Toyota RAV4 Plug-in Hybrid from Woodstock; Canadian-built CR-V/Civic/RX hybrids are not plug-ins, so nothing).
  • Origin rule: made in Canada or in a country with a Canadian free-trade agreement — Chinese-built vehicles are excluded regardless of price (Shanghai Model 3, Polestar 2, Lincoln Nautilus, future BYD).
  • One rebate per person for the whole five-year program; amounts step down to $4,000/$2,000 in 2027, $3,000/$1,500 in 2028–29 and $2,000/$1,000 in 2030. Budget of the program is a five-year federal commitment, no first-come funding cliff announced as of Sept 2026.
  • Eligible examples under the cap: Kia EV4 (all trims), Kia EV5 Light/Wind FWD, Chevrolet Bolt, Equinox EV LT FWD, Nissan Leaf S+, Hyundai Kona Electric Preferred, Fiat 500e, Toyota C-HR SE and bZ XLE, Mustang Mach-E Select, Volvo EX30, Tesla Model Y RWD (Berlin-built), Subaru Uncharted FWD; PHEVs: Prius Plug-in, Tucson/Sportage/Sorento/Niro PHEV, Outlander PHEV ES, Escape PHEV leftovers, Pacifica PHEV (no cap).
  • The federal EV Availability Standard (sales mandate) was repealed on 5 Feb 2026; no federal purchase penalty or road-user charge exists.

Provincial/territorial:

  • Quebec — Roulez vert: 2026 amounts $2,000 BEV/FCEV, $1,000 PHEV with ≥ 15 kWh battery, $500 PHEV 8–15 kWh, $1,000 used BEV, MSRP cap $65,000 (base trim), stackable with EVAP; the program ends 1 Jan 2027 (no rebate for vehicles registered after 31 Dec 2026). Quebec is also introducing an annual EV registration surcharge (~$125) from 2027 and keeps its EV exemption from the 1 % luxury registration fee up to $75,000.
  • British Columbia: the passenger CleanBC Go Electric rebate is gone (paused May 2025, formally scrapped in 2026); only the fleet/commercial program (Class 2b–8, reopened 10 Aug 2026), BC Hydro home-charger rebates ($350) and the ZEV PST thresholds (+$20,000, section 2.4) remain. No PST exemption on new ZEVs.
  • Prince Edward Island: $4,000 BEV / $2,000 PHEV (new or used, Tesla excluded), plus $1,000 for a Level 2 charger; stacks to $9,000 with EVAP.
  • Yukon: $5,000 BEV/PHEV (up to $7,500 for some classes), $1,500 charger rebate; stacks with EVAP. Northwest Territories: $5,000 (Arctic Energy Alliance, funding pauses). Nunavut: none.
  • Nova Scotia ($3,000, ended Apr 2025), New Brunswick ($5,000, ended Jul 2025), Newfoundland & Labrador ($2,500, ended 15 Mar 2026), Manitoba ($4,000, ended 31 Mar 2026): no longer available.
  • Ontario, Alberta, Saskatchewan: no purchase rebates; Ontario utilities and Hydro-Québec/BC Hydro offer $350–$1,000 charger rebates. Ontario's Ultra-Low Overnight electricity plan (2.8 ¢/kWh 11 pm–7 am) is the best deal for home charging in the country.
  • Employer/fleet: 100 % first-year capital-cost allowance (Class 54/55, $61,000 cap) for business EVs until end-2027 (phasing down).

4. Dealer discount climate, September 2026

Context: sales rose for a third straight month in August (168,000, +5.4 % YoY per DesRosiers) but DesRosiers expects September to be down on 2025 because of the escalating tariff war and an affordability squeeze (CPI ~3 % on fuel, Bank of Canada rate held at 2.25 % on 2 Sept). Gasoline is at $1.89/L and diesel a record $2.64/L after seven months of Strait of Hormuz closure, so hybrids and EVs are hot and V8 trucks/SUVs are soft. EVs plus hybrids are ~64 % of Toyota Canada's retail volume; the federal $5,000 rebate has made sub-$50k EVs the fastest-moving stock. Inventory is normal for most brands except Toyota/Lexus hybrids and Canadian-built RAV4.

Loyalty/conquest bonuses of $500–1,500 and 0.5–1 % rate cuts are common.

Chinese-brand price pressure has not arrived yet (BYD stores open late 2026), but the Shanghai-built Tesla Model 3 at $39,490 and the $38,995 Kia EV4 have already forced Chevrolet, Nissan and Hyundai to discount sub-$50k EVs.

5. Running costs, September 2026

  • Gasoline (regular 87): national average $1.89/L (29 Sept 2026, down from $1.91 the week before). Cities: Vancouver ~$1.92 (Metro Vancouver 18.5 ¢ TransLink tax; peaked at $1.89 in July), Toronto ~$1.80, Montreal ~$1.86, Calgary ~$1.68 (Alberta $1.68 is the cheapest province), Halifax ~$1.93, St. John's ~$2.20 (NL is dearest), Whitehorse ~$2.11. Premium 91 adds 20–25 ¢/L. The consumer carbon charge was abolished 1 Apr 2025, and the federal excise tax (10 ¢/L gasoline, 4 ¢/L diesel) has been suspended since 20 Apr 2026, extended to 31 Jan 2027 (half rate Feb–Mar 2027) — budget for +10 ¢ when it returns.
  • Diesel: $2.64/L national average (record, 29 Sept 2026), ~60 % above pre-war levels; $2.45–2.90 by region. Diesel pickups and SUVs now cost more per km than gasoline hybrids.
  • Home electricity (residential, all-in per kWh): Quebec 7.4–10.7 ¢ (Hydro-Québec Rate D, cheapest), Manitoba ~10 ¢, BC 9.6 ¢ (Step 1) / 15.1 ¢ (Step 2), Ontario 7.6 ¢ off-peak / 12.2 mid / 18.2 ¢ on-peak, Ultra-Low Overnight 2.8 ¢ (11 pm–7 am), Alberta 12–18 ¢ (regulated/variable + delivery), Saskatchewan 15.8–17.1 ¢, New Brunswick 13–15 ¢, NL 12.9 ¢, PEI 16.6 ¢, Nova Scotia 17.6 ¢ (dearest). Use ~11 ¢ for Ontario overnight charging and 8 ¢ for Quebec in the calculator. Public Level 2 20–30 ¢/kWh; DC fast 35–55 ¢/kWh (Tesla Supercharger, Electrify Canada, Petro-Canada, FLO, Circuit électrique 45 ¢/kWh).
  • Annual distance: 15,000 km is the national average (Statistics Canada/insurance underwriting standard); Alberta/Saskatchewan 18,000–20,000, Quebec/BC urban 12,000–14,000.
  • Financing: Bank of Canada policy rate 2.25 %, prime 4.45 %. Bank auto loans 6.5–7.5 % (national average 6.57 %), dealer/captive standard rates 5.99–8.99 %, subsidised captive rates 0–3.99 % on selected models (0 % over 60–84 months at GM, Ram, VW, Nissan, Cadillac; 0.99–2.99 % at Honda, Mazda, Subaru, Kia, Ford), sub-prime 12.9–29.99 %. Terms of 72–84 months are the norm (average new-car loan ≈ 80 months; 96 months exists); leasing (36–48 months, 16,000–24,000 km/yr) is 25–30 % of retail deals and dominates luxury brands. Typical down payment 10 %; negative equity rolled into the next loan is common.
  • Ownership extras: winter tires are mandatory in Quebec 1 Dec–15 Mar and on BC mountain highways Oct–Apr ($1,000–2,500 with rims); Quebec plates renew every year with the SAAQ bill; Ontario has no renewal fee but requires renewal every 1–2 years.

6. Glossary (Canada)

  • MSRP / PDSF: manufacturer's suggested retail price — the sticker before freight, fees and taxes; the number used in this dataset.
  • Freight & PDI (transport et préparation): the manufacturer's fixed delivery and pre-delivery inspection charge ($1,850–4,000), added to every sale and taxed.
  • All-in price / prix tout inclus: the advertised price ON, QC, BC, AB, MB and SK dealers must show — MSRP + freight + all fees, excluding only sales tax and registration.
  • A/C tax: the $100 federal excise tax on vehicle air conditioners.
  • Green levy: federal excise tax of $1,000–4,000 on cars/SUVs/vans rated 13 L/100 km or worse (weighted city/highway); pickups exempt.
  • Luxury tax (Select Luxury Items Tax): federal tax on vehicles over $100,000 — the lesser of 10 % of the price or 20 % of the amount above $100,000, with GST/HST charged on top.
  • HST / GST / PST / QST / RST: federal-provincial Harmonized Sales Tax (ON 13 %, NS 14 %, NB/PE/NL 15 %); 5 % federal Goods and Services Tax; provincial sales tax (BC 7–20 %, SK 6 %, MB 7 % RST, QC 9.975 % QST).
  • BC luxury PST: the higher PST rates (8–20 %) BC charges on passenger vehicles from $55,000 ($75,000 for ZEVs).
  • Counter-tariff / surtax: Canada's 25 % duty on US-assembled vehicles (April 2025–), remitted for automakers with Canadian plants; and the 6.1 %-in-quota / 100 %-above-quota regime for Chinese-built EVs.
  • EVAP: the federal Electric Vehicle Affordability Program — $5,000 (BEV) / $2,500 (PHEV) at the dealer for vehicles under $50,000 all-in, one per person, 2026–2030.
  • Roulez vert: Quebec's EV rebate ($2,000 BEV / $1,000 PHEV in 2026, ends 31 Dec 2026).
  • iZEV: the old federal $5,000 rebate that ran out in January 2025 (you will still see it on 2025 window stickers).
  • Admin / doc fee: the dealer's own paperwork charge ($199–999), negotiable and included in all-in prices.
  • OMVIC / AMVIC / VSA / OPC: the dealer regulators of Ontario, Alberta, BC and Quebec; OMVIC collects $22 per sale.
  • Autopac / SGI Auto Fund / ICBC Autoplan / SAAQ: the public insurance schemes of Manitoba, Saskatchewan, BC and (injury only) Quebec, bought with the plate.
  • Residual / lease-end value, kilometre allowance: leasing terms — the buy-back price and the 16,000–24,000 km/yr cap.
  • NRCan rating / EnerGuide label: Canada's official fuel-consumption and range figures (city/highway/combined L/100 km, Le/100 km and km for EVs), printed on every new car.
  • CUSMA/USMCA-compliant: a vehicle meeting the North American content rules; only the non-Canadian/Mexican share of its value carries the 25 % counter-tariff.

Motorcycles and scooters

Price basis: Maker's published price for each variant, on the basis used locally. Compiled: 10 October 2026 from Maker price lists and dealer listings in this market not linked

How these numbers work

List price is the maker’s published price for each model’s cheapest variant through its most expensive, on the basis used locally (MSRP before freight and setup), compiled in early October 2026. Rows marked Estimate use estimated or unconfirmed pricing; Listed rows are the maker’s published price on the compile date.

Estimated ride-away applies the discount typical for that model’s demand, then adds freight and setup where they are charged, the sales, purchase or road tax for your region, registration and plates, and the compulsory insurance where it is bought with the bike. Full insurance is shown as information. Dealer-fitted extras and accessories are not included.

Fuel economy is the maker’s or the official combined figure; electric range is the claimed figure for the biggest battery. Licence classes, learner restrictions and city bans are noted per model where they matter.

Fee lines (data/vfees.json → CA.moto)

Local terms: Freight & PDI: The maker’s fixed delivery and pre-delivery inspection charge, C$500 to C$1,500 on a motorcycle, added to every sale and taxed. M1 / M2 / M (Ontario): Graduated licensing: a written test for M1, a road test for M2 after 60 days, and a second road test for the full M. SAAQ / ICBC / MPI: Quebec, BC and Manitoba sell injury (or full) insurance with the plate, so their registration bills are much higher than Ontario’s.

Boats and jet skis

Price basis: Maker's published price for the standard package. Compiled: 10 October 2026 from Maker price lists and dealer listings in this market not linked

How these numbers work

List price is the brand’s published price for each model’s cheapest standard package through its most expensive, compiled in early October 2026. In this market many boats are priced as boat-motor-trailer packages and others as a hull with the engine extra; the note on each row says which. Rows marked Estimate use estimated or unconfirmed pricing; Listed rows are the maker’s published price on the compile date.

Estimated on-the-water applies the discount typical for that model, then adds freight and dealer preparation where they are charged, the sales or purchase tax for your region (with the caps some places apply to boats), registration and titling, and the compulsory insurance where any exists. Trailer registration, slips, mooring and full insurance are shown as information.

Fuel use is a typical cruise figure in gallons or litres per hour for the standard engine; the yearly fuel estimate multiplies it by the engine hours you set in the cost assumptions.

PCOC: The Pleasure Craft Operator Card, a lifetime boating-safety certificate required for anyone driving a motorised boat. Luxury tax: The federal Select Luxury Items Tax on boats over C$250,000, charged on top of GST and provincial tax.

Planes, jets and helicopters

Price basis: Manufacturer factory list price, base to typically equipped, 2026. Compiled: 12 October 2026 from Manufacturer price lists and type announcements not linked · GAMA shipment and billing reports · AIN (Aviation International News) · Flying Magazine

How these numbers work

Factory list price is the maker’s 2026 price for each type from the base configuration to a typically equipped one, compiled in October 2026 from manufacturer price lists, type announcements and the trade press. Prices are the maker’s dollar or euro list converted at ECB reference rates of 12 Oct 2026 and shown before VAT, GST and duty, so every row is marked Converted: the invoice is in the maker’s currency and moves with the exchange rate. Business jets and helicopters are quoted typically equipped; the spread in each row covers the usual options.

Estimated delivered applies the discount or premium typical for that type (delivery positions on hot types trade above list; slow sellers and end-of-run types go for 5% to 12% under), then adds delivery and acceptance where the maker charges it, the sales tax, VAT, GST or duty for your region with the exemptions and caps that apply to aircraft, and registration with the national authority. Insurance, hangarage, the annual inspection and a pilot licence are shown as information, since they dwarf the purchase taxes over the years you own it.

Fuel burn is the maker’s cruise figure in gallons or litres per hour; the yearly fuel estimate multiplies it by the flight hours you set in the cost assumptions at local avgas and Jet-A prices. Speed and range are the maker’s long-range cruise figures in knots and nautical miles, with km or miles alongside.

Local terms: Factory list vs typically equipped: Makers quote a base price and a "typically equipped" price with the options most buyers take; the row’s range spans both. Delivery position: New aircraft are sold as positions in the production line; a hot type means a wait of one to three years, and positions trade at a premium. Pre-buy / acceptance inspection: An independent inspection before you accept delivery, even on a new aircraft; catches squawks the factory should fix at its cost. Registration: Every aircraft is registered with the national authority (FAA N-number, CAA G-register, EASA state registers, DGCA VT-, CAAC B-); the fee is small, the paperwork is not. Owner-flown: Aircraft a licensed private pilot can fly without a professional crew: pistons, most turboprops and a few single-pilot jets (Vision Jet, Citation M2, Phenom 100). eVTOL: Electric vertical take-off aircraft; the EHang EH216-S is certified in China and ultralight single-seaters fly in the US, while the air-taxi designs (Joby, Archer, VX4) are still in certification and not sold to individuals. LSA / MOSAIC: Light-sport aircraft: simple two-seaters flown on a sport-pilot licence; the FAA’s 2025 MOSAIC rule lets heavier four-seaters qualify. TBO: Time between overhauls of the engine, 1,500 to 2,200 hours on pistons; an overhaul costs US$30,000 to US$60,000 and is part of the running cost.

Mexico MX

Cars and trucks

Price basis: Precio de lista, IVA and ISAN included. Compiled: 29 September 2026 from Autocosmos catálogo · Manufacturer sites not linked · SAT, Anexo 15 RMF 2026 (ISAN)

How these numbers work

Precio de lista is the brand’s published price, IVA and ISAN included, for each model’s cheapest versión through its most expensive, from brand sites and Autocosmos in September 2026. Promotional “precios especiales” tied to financing are noted, not used.

Estimated precio final applies the discount typical for that model (nothing on Versa, Hilux or RAV4; MX$10,000 to MX$30,000 bonos on most; 6% to 15% on Chinese brands and slow sellers), then adds your state’s alta de placas, the holograma for Megalópolis states, and first-year tenencia where it still applies (3% of the pre-IVA value in CDMX and Edomex above MX$638,000; EVs and hybrids exempt). Comprehensive insurance is shown but not included.

Rendimiento comes from the CONUEE Ecovehículos label; EV range is as published by the brand (NEDC/CLTC for Chinese brands, EPA for Tesla, GM and Ford). Cars built in China carry a 50% import tariff since January 2026, absorbed so far by the brands.

Regions in the picker: Ciudad de México · Estado de México · Jalisco · Nuevo León · Guanajuato · Puebla · Querétaro · Veracruz · Yucatán · Baja California · Chihuahua · Otro estado (default Ciudad de México). The rates, brackets and formulas for each are in the research note below.

Research note: Mexico (mx) — on-road math and market notes, late September 2026 (27 sources)

Currency MXN (pesos, "$"). Prices in data/cars_mx.json are integer pesos, the precio de lista (also "precio sugerido al público", "MSRP") that the brand publishes for each version, cheapest and dearest version of each nameplate, excluding paint, accessories and dealer add-ons. Efficiency columns: combined consumption in L/100 km converted from the km/L figure on the CONUEE/PROFECO "Ecovehículos" label where one exists (l100 = 100 ÷ km/L; the label is derived from EPA-style lab cycles, so real-world use is 10–25 % worse), CO2 g/km approximated from consumption (Mexico has no CO2-based tax, so this column is informational), EV/PHEV range as the brand publishes it in Mexico (Chinese brands quote NEDC or CLTC, Tesla/Ford/GM quote EPA, Europeans quote WLTP; the cycle is named in each note where it matters).

1. What the precio de lista already includes

A Mexican list price includes 16 % IVA and the federal new-car tax ISAN. Volkswagen's September 2026 footnote is typical: *"El precio aquí establecido corresponde a operaciones de contado en Moneda Nacional… las cuales incluyen el 16 % de Impuesto al Valor Agregado (IVA) y el Impuesto Sobre Autos Nuevos (ISAN cuando aplique)."* ISAN is legally paid by the manufacturer/dealer and passed through inside the price; SAT rules say it is not broken out as a separate line on the CFDI invoice (it is buried in the subtotal, and IVA is charged on the ISAN-inclusive amount). So:

  • list = (P + ISAN(P)) × 1.16, where P is the pre-tax "precio de enajenación" (the ISAN base). To recover P from a list price solve P + ISAN(P) = list / 1.16 (iterate the bracket, or just use P ≈ list / 1.16 − ISAN).
  • The list price excludes: state registration (alta de placas / tarjeta de circulación), state tenencia where it applies, insurance, the verification hologram, and any dealer "gestoría" (paperwork) fee or forced accessories. There is no delivery/freight charge in Mexico; "precio de lista" is the same nationwide (border-zone IVA of 8 % only applies to some services, not to cars).
  • IVA on a car is 16 % everywhere in Mexico. The 8 % border-region IVA does not apply to vehicle sales.
  • Rule of thumb: drive-away ≈ list × 1.00–1.01 plus insurance for anything under $638,000 in CDMX/Edomex (tenencia subsidised, ISAN already inside), and list × 1.03–1.04 plus insurance for a $1M+ petrol car in a tenencia state.

2. From list price to on-the-road

2.1 ISAN — Impuesto Sobre Automóviles Nuevos (federal, inside the list price)

Base = pre-tax price P (with factory options, before discounts, without IVA). Tariff for 2026 (SAT Anexo 15, RMF 2026, DOF 28-Dec-2025; amounts are inflation-indexed every January):

Límite inferior (P)Límite superiorCuota fija% sobre excedente
$0.01$383,940.35$0.002 %
$383,940.36$460,728.35$7,678.675 %
$460,728.36$537,516.64$11,518.2510 %
$537,516.65$691,092.34$19,197.0415 %
$691,092.35onward$42,233.3517 %
  • Reduction for expensive cars: if P > $1,060,189.93, subtract 7 % of (P − 1,060,189.93) from the tax computed with the table (so the marginal rate above that point is effectively 10 %).
  • Exemptions (Art. 8-II, 2026 amounts): P ≤ $356,934.05 → no ISAN at all; P between $356,934.06 and $452,116.48 → pay 50 % of the computed ISAN; above → 100 %. In list-price terms: full exemption up to roughly $414,000 list, the half-rate band up to roughly $530,000 list.
  • Electric, plug-in and hybrid cars pay no ISAN (Art. 8-IV: battery-electric vehicles and "eléctricos que además cuenten con motor de combustión interna", i.e. HEVs and PHEVs, plus hydrogen). Brands do not always treat 48 V mild hybrids as exempt; assume mild hybrids (Suzuki Boostergreen, Mazda MHEV, Peugeot 3008 "hybrid") pay ISAN unless the dealer confirms otherwise.
  • Trucks: chassis-cab and cargo trucks up to 4,250 kg payload and panel vans with ≤ 3 seats pay a flat 5 % (Art. 3-II); double-cab pickups with 5 seats are "automóviles" and use the table.

`` ISAN(P): if EV/PHEV/HEV: 0 if P <= 356934.05: 0 t = P<=383940.35 ? 0.02*P : P<=460728.35 ? 7678.67 + 0.05*(P-383940.35) : P<=537516.64 ? 11518.25 + 0.10*(P-460728.35) : P<=691092.34 ? 19197.04 + 0.15*(P-537516.64) : 42233.35 + 0.17*(P-691092.34) if P > 1060189.93: t -= 0.07*(P-1060189.93) if P <= 452116.48: t *= 0.5 return t `` Worked: P = $500,000 → $11,518.25 + 10 % × 39,271.65 = $15,445 (3.1 %); P = $1,000,000 → $94,748 (9.5 %); P = $2,000,000 → 264,748 − 65,787 = $198,961 (9.9 %). Remember these amounts are already in the sticker; the only time a buyer meets ISAN live is on dealer-fitted accessories invoiced with the car.

2.2 Tenencia — state ownership tax (the big regional variable)

Mexico abolished the federal tenencia in 2012; each state decides. In 2026 roughly half the states still levy it, almost all with a subsidy for cheaper cars paid on time. Where it exists the formula is the old federal one:

  • New car: tenencia = 3 % × valor total, where valor total = price without IVA but including ISAN and import duties (≈ list ÷ 1.16), prorated by month of purchase (factor: Jan 1.00, Feb 0.92, Mar 0.83, Apr 0.75, May 0.67, Jun 0.58, Jul 0.50, Aug 0.42, Sep 0.33, Oct 0.25, Nov 0.17, Dec 0.08).
  • Following years: 3 % × valor total × depreciation factor: year 1 = 0.850, 2 = 0.725, 3 = 0.600, 4 = 0.500, 5 = 0.400, 6 = 0.300, 7 = 0.225, 8 = 0.150, 9+ = 0.075 (CDMX Código Fiscal art. 161 BIS 13; Edomex uses the same ladder).
  • Pay with the annual refrendo (plate renewal) in January–March; late payment loses the subsidy and adds ~1.5 %/month surcharges.

State rules for the suggested dropdown (2026):

Programmer formula (CDMX/Edomex): `` valor = list / 1.16 // pre-IVA, ISAN inside tenencia_year0 = (EV or hybrid) ? 0 : (list <= 638000 && paidOnTime) ? 0 : 0.03 * valor * monthFactor[purchaseMonth] tenencia_yearN = 0.03 * valor * dep[N] (same exemption test on the depreciated value incl. IVA) `` Worked: Tiguan R-Line, list $795,790, bought in CDMX in October 2026 → valor $686,026 → full-year tenencia $20,581, 2026 prorated $5,145; 2027: $17,494; 2028: $14,921. A Sealion 7 at $949,800 pays $0 (EV).

2.3 Verification, holograms and Hoy No Circula (Megalópolis: CDMX, Edomex, Hidalgo, Morelos, Puebla, Tlaxcala, Querétaro)
  • Every petrol/diesel car verifies its emissions twice a year; CDMX price for H2 2026 is $765 per visit (Edomex ≈ $730).
  • New cars get the "00" hologram, obtained once at a verificentro within 180 days of the invoice ($765): no further verification and exempt from Hoy No Circula for 2 years, then hologram "0" (one verification a year, still exempt from the weekday ban) while the car is under ~8 years old and passes.
  • EVs and hybrids get the "E" (exento) hologram: no verification, permanently exempt from Hoy No Circula and from the "doble Hoy No Circula" smog-alert bans; also 50 % off CDMX plates ($486). This, plus zero ISAN and zero tenencia, is the real EV/hybrid incentive package in the capital.
  • Outside the Megalópolis most states have no verification programme at all (Nuevo León, Jalisco, Guanajuato and Baja California have voluntary or loosely enforced schemes).
2.4 Insurance

Not compulsory nationwide except third-party liability on federal highways and in some states (Baja California demands it to renew plates), but any financed car must carry cobertura amplia (comprehensive) named to the lender. Typical annual premiums in 2026 (miituo/SI.MX quotes, CDMX, 35–45-year-old driver):

  • Limitada (theft + liability): $6,000–$9,600 a year.
  • Amplia: $8,800–$18,000 for a $300k–$500k car, roughly 2.5–4 % of value; $20,000–$35,000 (3–4 %) for a $700k–$1M SUV; 4–6 % for the most-stolen models (Versa, NP300/Frontier, Aveo, Hilux, Tsuru-era pickups) and for Chinese brands/EVs where parts are slow; premium brands above $1.5M about 2.5–3 %. Edomex and Puebla quote 10–20 % above CDMX; Guadalajara/Monterrey/Mérida cheaper. Deductibles 5 % (damage) and 10 % (theft) are the norm.
  • Budget a one-year comprehensive premium of 3.5 % of list as the default in the calculator.
2.5 Dealer fees and add-ons
  • "Gastos de gestoría" (dealer does the plates for you): $1,500–$6,000, negotiable; you can plate the car yourself for the state fee only.
  • Dealers push "seguro de la agencia" (often 10–20 % dearer than online quotes), GAP insurance ($3,000–$8,000), "garantía extendida" and accessory packs (film, alarm, mats: $5,000–$20,000). None is mandatory.
  • Financing "comisión por apertura" 1–3 % of the amount financed (some captives 0 %).

2.7 Regions for the dropdown (suggest 12)

Ciudad de México · Estado de México · Jalisco · Nuevo León · Guanajuato · Puebla · Querétaro · Veracruz · Yucatán · Baja California · Chihuahua · Otro estado. CDMX and Edomex share the $638,000 cap but differ on hybrids (CDMX exempts all hybrids, Edomex only < 100 g/km) and refrendo ($760 vs $990); Nuevo León is the "no tenencia but $4,106 refrendo" case; Querétaro and Guanajuato show the high-value-only pattern; Veracruz/Puebla the "free if you pay by March" pattern; Chihuahua/BC the north-border high-refrendo, no-tenencia pattern.

3. EV / hybrid incentives in force (September 2026)

Federal:

  • ISAN exemption for BEVs, PHEVs, HEVs and hydrogen cars (worth 2–10 % of the pre-tax price; e.g. ≈ $70,000 on a $900,000 SUV).
  • Import tariff: since 1 January 2026 cars from countries without a trade agreement (China above all, also India-built cars from some brands) pay 50 % duty (up from 20 %); the earlier 0 % tariff for Chinese EVs ended in 2024. Brands stocked up in Q4 2025, so the visible price effect was small in H1 2026 and is expected to show in late-2026/2027 model years. Cars built in Mexico, the US/Canada (T-MEC), the EU, Japan, Korea, Brazil/Argentina (ACE-55) and Thailand (no: Thailand has no FTA, but Toyota/Isuzu pickups enter under special programmes) are not affected.
  • ISR/depreciation: companies may depreciate EVs/PHEVs up to $250,000 (vs $175,000 for ICE) per year; irrelevant to private buyers.
  • No federal purchase rebate exists. Home-charger installation has no subsidy; CFE will install a separate meter for a charger on request.

State/city:

  • CDMX: tenencia 0 % for EVs and hybrids; plates at half price ($486); hologram E (no verification, no Hoy No Circula); some free/discounted parking meters.
  • Edomex: tenencia exempt for EVs and for hybrids under 100 g/km CO2; hologram E.
  • Puebla, Guanajuato, Querétaro, Hidalgo, Veracruz: EVs/hybrids exempt from tenencia (where charged) or from the verification programme.
  • Nuevo León: no exemption; EVs actually pay 17.5 UMA refrendo (≈ $2,053) versus 35 UMA for petrol cars, a half-price concession.
  • Jalisco: EVs get a free "placa verde" and free plate exchange; no tenencia anyway.
  • Public charging: Tesla Superchargers, VEMO, Evergo, BYD and dealer networks charge $8–12 per kWh on DC fast chargers; CFE home rates are the ones in section 5.

4. Dealer discount climate, September 2026

Context: the market runs at about 1.45–1.5 million units a year (2025 record 1.55 M, 2026 flat to slightly down); Nissan, Chevrolet, VW, Toyota and Kia lead; Chinese brands hold about 20–22 % of sales (BYD, MG, Chirey/Omoda/Jaecoo, GWM, Geely, Changan, JAC) and the 50 % tariff has not yet pushed their prices up because they absorbed it and cut bonos instead of list prices. Financing promos matter more than list discounts in Mexico: the "precio especial con crédito" (Nissan V-Drive $45,000 off, Chirey $30,000–$50,000 "precio especial", MG loyalty bonos $30,000) is how discounting is done.

SegmentTypical discount off listNotes
Hot (0–2 %): Nissan Versa/NP300, Toyota Hilux 2027, RAV4, CX-5 2026, Kia Seltos 2027, Suzuki Jimny, Tesla, Porsche 911/718, Land Rover Defender/Range Rover, G-Class, BYD KingNothing, or a free accessory packWaiting lists on Jimny, RAV4 PHEV, Hilux diesel auto, GR models

5. Running costs and finance (September 2026)

  • Fuel, national averages 27 Sep 2026 (CNE/PROFECO daily report): Magna (regular, 87 oct) $23.69/L; Premium (91–92 oct) $28.58/L; Diesel $27.01/L. CDMX $23.81 / $28.80 / $26.97; Edomex $23.65 / $28.16 / $26.75; the north border (Tijuana, Ciudad Juárez) runs $1–2 cheaper because of the IEPS border stimulus. The voluntary "Magna at $24 max" agreement between the government and retailers (Feb 2025) still holds. LPG/CNG conversions are rare on new cars; ignore.
  • Electricity (CFE, 2026 tariffs): Tarifa 1 (subsidised domestic) basic block $0.987/kWh, intermediate $1.198/kWh, excess $3.218/kWh; a home that charges an EV usually crosses the 250 kWh/month (Tarifa 1; 400 in 1B, 850 in 1C) average and drops into DAC at ≈ $6.20/kWh plus a fixed charge. Use $2.50/kWh as a blended home figure for an EV that adds ~250 kWh/month, $6.20/kWh for DAC households, $10/kWh for public fast charging.
  • Annual distance: 15,000 km is the standard assumption (AMIS/AMDA use 15,000–18,000; CDMX commuters run higher).
  • Loans: banks 12.99–15.5 % annual (BBVA 12.99 % for hybrids/EVs, 14.99 % conventional, 9.99 % with payroll; Banorte 13.49 %, Banco del Bajío 13.8 %, HSBC 13.9 %, Scotiabank 14.5 %, Santander 15.49 %), CAT 19–26 %; captive finance arms (NR Finance/CrediNissan, Toyota Financial, GM Financial, VW Financial, Ford Credit, Kia Finance) 16–18 % list rate but with subsidised promos of 0–9.9 % on selected models and terms (BYD/Denza via Banorte 7.88 %). Enganche 10–20 % (5 % minimum at some banks, 20 % for the best rate), plazos 12–72 months (60 is the norm), comisión por apertura 1–3 %, comprehensive insurance and life cover mandatory and usually financed into the loan. "Arrendamiento puro" (lease) is common above $700,000 for tax reasons.
  • Exchange rate context: about 18.3–18.8 MXN per USD through September 2026.

6. Glossary for a first-time buyer

  • Precio de lista / precio sugerido: the brand's published price for a version; already includes IVA and ISAN; excludes plates, tenencia, insurance.
  • IVA: 16 % value-added tax, inside every list price.
  • ISAN (Impuesto Sobre Automóviles Nuevos): federal progressive tax of 2–17 % on the pre-tax price, hidden in the list price; cars under ≈ $414,000 list, all EVs and all hybrids pay none.
  • Tenencia: annual state ownership tax, 3 % of the pre-IVA value depreciating each year; abolished in half the states and subsidised for cars under $638,000 in CDMX/Edomex.
  • Refrendo: the yearly plate-renewal fee ($700–$4,100 depending on the state) paid in Jan–Mar; paying it on time is what unlocks the tenencia subsidy.
  • Alta de placas / tarjeta de circulación: first registration and the registration card; $972 in CDMX, $1,194 in Edomex, done within 30 days.
  • Verificación / holograma 00, 0, 1, 2, E: the twice-yearly emissions test in the Megalópolis; 00 = new car exempt two years, E = electric/hybrid exempt forever.
  • Hoy No Circula: the weekday driving ban by plate digit in CDMX/Edomex; holograms 00, 0 and E are exempt.
  • Enganche: down payment, usually 10–20 %.
  • CAT (Costo Anual Total): the all-in annualised cost of a loan (interest + fees + insurance), the number to compare, typically 19–26 %.
  • Bono / precio especial: manufacturer or dealer cash discount, often conditional on financing with the captive lender.
  • Cobertura amplia / limitada: comprehensive vs theft-and-liability insurance; lenders require amplia.
  • Gestoría: the dealer's paperwork fee for plating the car; optional and negotiable.
  • Rendimiento (km/L): fuel economy is quoted in kilometres per litre from the CONUEE Ecovehículos label; 20 km/L = 5.0 L/100 km.
  • Hecho en México / importado: where the car is built decides the tariff exposure (Chinese-built cars carry the 50 % duty since 2026) and often the warranty logistics.
  • T-MEC: the USMCA trade agreement; cars built in Mexico/US/Canada cross tariff-free.

Motorcycles and scooters

Price basis: Maker's published price for each variant, on the basis used locally. Compiled: 10 October 2026 from Maker price lists and dealer listings in this market not linked

How these numbers work

List price is the maker’s published price for each model’s cheapest variant through its most expensive, on the basis used locally (the brand’s retail price with taxes inside), compiled in early October 2026. Rows marked Estimate use estimated or unconfirmed pricing; Listed rows are the maker’s published price on the compile date.

Estimated ride-away applies the discount typical for that model’s demand, then adds freight and setup where they are charged, the sales, purchase or road tax for your region, registration and plates, and the compulsory insurance where it is bought with the bike. Full insurance is shown as information. Dealer-fitted extras and accessories are not included.

Fuel economy is the maker’s or the official combined figure; electric range is the claimed figure for the biggest battery. Licence classes, learner restrictions and city bans are noted per model where they matter.

Boats and jet skis

Price basis: Maker's published price for the standard package. Compiled: 10 October 2026 from Maker price lists and dealer listings in this market not linked

How these numbers work

List price is the brand’s published price for each model’s cheapest standard package through its most expensive, compiled in early October 2026. In this market the price normally includes the standard engine; trailers, electronics and canvas are extra. Rows marked Estimate use estimated or unconfirmed pricing; Listed rows are the maker’s published price on the compile date.

Estimated on-the-water applies the discount typical for that model, then adds freight and dealer preparation where they are charged, the sales or purchase tax for your region (with the caps some places apply to boats), registration and titling, and the compulsory insurance where any exists. Trailer registration, slips, mooring and full insurance are shown as information.

Fuel use is a typical cruise figure in gallons or litres per hour for the standard engine; the yearly fuel estimate multiplies it by the engine hours you set in the cost assumptions.

Planes, jets and helicopters

Price basis: Manufacturer factory list price, base to typically equipped, 2026. Compiled: 12 October 2026 from Manufacturer price lists and type announcements not linked · GAMA shipment and billing reports · AIN (Aviation International News) · Flying Magazine

How these numbers work

Factory list price is the maker’s 2026 price for each type from the base configuration to a typically equipped one, compiled in October 2026 from manufacturer price lists, type announcements and the trade press. Prices are the maker’s dollar or euro list converted at ECB reference rates of 12 Oct 2026 and shown before VAT, GST and duty, so every row is marked Converted: the invoice is in the maker’s currency and moves with the exchange rate. Business jets and helicopters are quoted typically equipped; the spread in each row covers the usual options.

Estimated delivered applies the discount or premium typical for that type (delivery positions on hot types trade above list; slow sellers and end-of-run types go for 5% to 12% under), then adds delivery and acceptance where the maker charges it, the sales tax, VAT, GST or duty for your region with the exemptions and caps that apply to aircraft, and registration with the national authority. Insurance, hangarage, the annual inspection and a pilot licence are shown as information, since they dwarf the purchase taxes over the years you own it.

Fuel burn is the maker’s cruise figure in gallons or litres per hour; the yearly fuel estimate multiplies it by the flight hours you set in the cost assumptions at local avgas and Jet-A prices. Speed and range are the maker’s long-range cruise figures in knots and nautical miles, with km or miles alongside.
